LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

The contradiction lies between Testimony 4 ("The proof is the bloodstain we found here in the meeting room!") and Evidence 19 ("Blood was detected on the secret elevator. It looks like it dripped through to the floor below."). Testimony 4 asserts the bloodstain *in* the meeting room is the proof. Evidence 19 indicates blood was found on the elevator, suggesting it originated *elsewhere* and dripped down. This creates a spatial conflict: if the blood is on the elevator, it doesn't necessarily prove the crime occurred solely in the meeting room.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial reasoning rules apply**

The rule "If XXX passes through YYY and ZZZ is inside YYY and ZZZ overlaps XXX then ZZZ is pierced" doesn't apply here. The rule "If XXX is shot from YYY direction then the bullet comes from YYY direction" also doesn't apply. None of the other rules directly address the spatial relationship of blood dripping from one location to another.

**Step 3: N/A**

**Step 4: Spatial Reasoning**

The contradiction arises from a basic understanding of how fluids behave. Blood dripping from the elevator implies a source of blood *above* the meeting room. This contradicts the assertion that the bloodstain in the meeting room is the sole or primary piece of evidence proving the crime occurred there. The elevator blood suggests the crime might have involved movement between the storeroom (one floor above) and the meeting room.

**Step 5:** {"evidence": 19, "testimony": 4}

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

The contradiction lies between Evidence 7 (Bigg Building Pamphlet) and Testimony 8 (Kay's Recollections). Evidence 7 describes the Bigg Building viewing platform as having a cherry tree to the left and a candy stand to the right. Kay's testimony states she was pushed from the building by someone coming from beyond the concession stand. This implies the concession stand was *near* the edge of the platform. However, the pamphlet explicitly states the candy stand is to the right-most edge, and the cherry tree to the left. If the concession stand is beyond the right-most edge, it cannot be near the edge from which Kay was pushed. This creates a spatial contradiction: Kay's recollection places the attacker near an edge, while the building's layout (as per the pamphlet) places the candy stand at that edge.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial reasoning rules apply**

None of the provided spatial reasoning rules directly address this contradiction. The rules deal with containment, covering, piercing, surrounding, and disconnection, but not the relative positioning of objects within a defined space and the implications of a witness's account.

**Step 3: N/A**

**Step 4: Spatial Reasoning Used**

The contradiction is identified through basic spatial reasoning and understanding of relative positioning. The pamphlet provides a fixed layout of the viewing platform. Kay's testimony describes an event occurring relative to a specific feature (the concession stand). The contradiction arises because the pamphlet's layout makes it impossible for the concession stand to be in the location implied by Kay's testimony. This is a direct conflict between a described spatial arrangement and a reported event within that arrangement.

**Step 5:** {"evidence": 7, "testimony": 8}

It's possible she simply didn't *notice* anyone, but the lack of observation is spatially inconsistent with the act of escorting someone.

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

Ron claims he "dancingly descended upon the scene of the crime" (Testimony 3). This implies he was physically present at the Lordly Tailor shop. However, the story states that Lordly Tailor is "decently far away from any other location." This suggests a significant distance. The Security Camera Photo (Evidence 9) shows the storeroom entrance. If Ron was at Lordly Tailor, he would have needed to travel a considerable distance. The Key Card (Evidence 12) shows Ron used a key card to enter KB Security's CEO office at 1 AM, the night of the crime. This implies he was at KB Security, not Lordly Tailor.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial reasoning rules apply**

The rule "If XXX is in a different room from YYY then XXX cannot see YYY" is relevant. While it doesn't directly address the contradiction, it highlights the spatial separation. The rule "If XXX is in the same room as YYY then XXX can potentially see YYY" is also relevant.

**Step 3: If applicable rules exist, state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

The rules "If XXX is in the same room as YYY then XXX can potentially see YYY" and "If XXX is in a different room from YYY then XXX cannot see YYY" highlight the spatial contradiction. Ron's testimony (3) places him at Lordly Tailor, implying he could potentially see the Sacred Urn and other items there. However, the Key Card record (Evidence 12) places him at KB Security, a different location, making it impossible for him to be at Lordly Tailor at the same time.

**Step 4: If none of the listed rules apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The contradiction is identified through basic spatial reasoning. Ron's claim of being at Lordly Tailor is incompatible with the evidence of him using a key card at KB Security on the same night, given the stated distance between the two locations. It's spatially impossible to be in two distant locations simultaneously.

------------------------------
**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

Testimony 3 states: "Inside that monster costume over there..." referring to the Taurusaurus costume. Testimony 4 states: "...There's dirt inside the costume. It must have gotten there when the body was hidden inside." This implies the president's body was hidden *inside* the Taurusaurus costume. However, Evidence 5 states: "The body lying face-up on top of the dirt, and yet there's no dirt on the front of the body." This suggests the body was *on top of* the dirt, not inside something. This creates a spatial contradiction: the body is both inside the costume and on top of the dirt.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial reasoning rules apply**

The rule "If XXX is inside YYY and YYY is inside ZZZ then XXX is inside ZZZ" could potentially apply, but it doesn't directly address the contradiction. The core issue is the conflicting locations of the body relative to the costume and the dirt.

**Step 3: If applicable rules exist, state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

None of the provided rules directly resolve the spatial contradiction. The rules focus on relative positions (in front of, behind, covered by) and access (locked), but don't address the simultaneous presence of an object in two distinct locations.

**Step 4: If none of the listed rules apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The contradiction is identified through basic spatial reasoning. Testimony 3 and 4 describe the body being *inside* the costume, while Evidence 5 describes the body being *on top of* the dirt. These are mutually exclusive spatial relationships. An object cannot simultaneously be inside one container and on top of a separate surface.

However, Simeon's testimony also states that the double told Gavelle to leave ahead of him. This creates a contradiction: if Simeon saw the double enter the hatch, and the double told Gavelle to leave, then Gavelle would have had to be present on the roof with Simeon and the double. But the security footage shows no evidence of Gavelle leaving the roof.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial reasoning rules apply**

**Step 3: If applicable rules exist, explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

No rules directly resolve the contradiction.

**Step 4: If none of the listed rules apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The contradiction arises from the spatial relationship of the rooftop, the hatch to the 51st floor, and the presence of individuals. Simeon's testimony places the "double" on the roof with him and Gavelle, and then entering the hatch. The security footage contradicts this by showing no record of Gavelle leaving the roof. This creates a spatial inconsistency: the double's actions (telling Gavelle to leave and then entering the hatch) are incompatible with the security footage's record of Gavelle's presence.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial reasoning rules** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable rules exist,** explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed rules apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

## CRITICAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT:
**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

The contradiction lies between Testimony 4 ("A shot! The bullet hit the man pulling the stand from the front, square in the forehead!") and Evidence 11 (Meraktis's Autopsy Report: "Cause of death: Single bullet to the right temple."). A bullet entering the right temple would mean the victim was hit on the right side, not the front. This contradicts Stickler's testimony that the victim was hit from the front.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial reasoning rules apply**

The rule "If XXX is hit from the front then XXX falls backward" is relevant. If the victim was hit from the front, he should have fallen backward. However, the autopsy report doesn't mention the direction of the fall, only the entry point of the bullet. The rule "If XXX is hit from behind then XXX falls forward" is also relevant, but doesn't directly address the contradiction.

**Step 3: If applicable rules exist, state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

The rule "If XXX is hit from the front then XXX falls backward" is relevant. Stickler's testimony states the victim was hit from the front. If this were true, the autopsy report should have indicated a backward fall. The fact that the autopsy report only mentions the bullet's entry point into the right temple, and not the direction of the fall, creates a spatial contradiction with Stickler's testimony.

**Step 4: If none of the listed rules apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The spatial reasoning used is based on the typical trajectory of a body after being shot. A shot to the forehead from the front would logically cause the body to fall backward. The autopsy report's lack of mention of the fall direction, combined with the specific location of the bullet entry (right temple), contradicts the claim of a frontal shot.

**Step 5:** {"evidence": 11, "testimony": 4}

{
  "evidence": 11,
  "testimony": 4

**Step 2: Check if any spatial reasoning rules apply**

If the rocks obstructed the view of the parking lot from the south, it's plausible they also limited her view of the area immediately beyond the broken end of the bridge. Testimony 6 suggests the victim ran a considerable distance (10 yards) before being stabbed. If Melissa was taking the photo from the south, and the rocks obstructed her view, it would be difficult for her to accurately assess the distance the victim ran. The photo places her near the broken end, while the testimony implies a much greater distance.

**Step 4: If none of the listed rules apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The contradiction is identified through basic spatial reasoning and understanding of perspective. The photo provides a snapshot of a specific location (near the broken end). The testimony describes an event (the victim running) that implies movement away from that location. The combination of these two pieces of information creates a spatial inconsistency. The rocks obstructing the view further complicate the situation, making it less likely Melissa could accurately judge the distance.

The rule "If XXX is hit from the front then XXX falls backward" could be relevant. If Smith was hit from the front, he would fall backward. The upside-down prints on the bottle could indicate Wright was behind Smith when he struck the blow, and then perhaps moved or fell after the act.

**Step 3: If applicable rules exist, explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

The rule "If XXX is hit from the front then XXX falls backward" applies. If Smith fell backward, and Wright's prints are upside-down on the murder weapon, it suggests Wright was behind Smith when the blow was delivered. Testimony 5 implies Wright grabbed the bottle *after* losing, which would likely result in normal hand placement and prints. The upside-down prints contradict this implication.

**Step 4: If none of the listed rules apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The spatial reasoning involves understanding the implications of hand placement and the direction of force. Normal hand placement leaves normal prints. An unusual hand position, like holding a bottle while falling or being moved, leaves upside-down prints. The testimony suggests a straightforward action (grabbing the bottle), while the evidence suggests a more complex scenario involving a fall or movement after the act.

Evidence 5 (Crime Scene Diagram) shows the corridor to the Fox Chamber (where the demon presumably was imprisoned) is to the *east*, not to the right. Therefore, L'Belle's description of his escape route contradicts the established spatial layout of the scene.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial reasoning rules apply**

The rule "If XXX blocks the path from YYY to ZZZ then YYY cannot reach ZZZ directly" is relevant. If L'Belle fled down the hallway to the right (as he claims), and that hallway leads to a dead end, then he could not have seen the demon emerge from the Fox Chamber (to the east) directly.

**Step 3: If applicable rules exist, state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

The rule "If XXX blocks the path from YYY to ZZZ then YYY cannot reach ZZZ directly" applies. In this case, the dead-end hallway (XXX) blocks the path from L'Belle (YYY) to the Fox Chamber (ZZZ). This contradicts L'Belle's claim of seeing the demon emerge from its prison *after* he fled down that hallway. It suggests his account of the events and the spatial layout are inconsistent.

**Step 4: If none of the listed rules apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The contradiction is identified through basic spatial reasoning. The Crime Scene Diagram establishes the location of the Fox Chamber relative to the hallway. L'Belle's testimony provides a description of his escape route. Comparing these two pieces of information reveals an inconsistency in the spatial relationships described.

------------------------------
**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

Testimony 3 states, "...the only person in this lounge the entire time was you, Miles Edgeworth!" This implies Edgeworth was continuously present in the lounge from the time the victim called for an attendant until his body was found. However, Evidence 9 (Ms. Meele's Testimony) states, "Mr. Akbey Hicks was there in his seat when we took off again at 5 AM." This means Hicks was in his seat, not the lounge, at 5 AM. Since the body was found at 6:15 AM, if Edgeworth was the *only* person in the lounge the entire time, he would have had to be there before 5 AM, contradicting the fact that Hicks was still in his seat at that time.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial reasoning rules apply**

The rule "If XXX is in the same room as YYY then XXX can potentially see YYY" is relevant. However, it doesn't directly address the contradiction. The rule "If XXX is inside YYY then YYY is contains XXX" is also relevant, but doesn't directly address the contradiction.

**Step 3: If applicable rules exist, state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

The rule "If XXX is in the same room as YYY then XXX can potentially see YYY" is relevant. Testimony 3 implies Edgeworth was in the lounge, and therefore could potentially see Hicks. However, Evidence 9 states Hicks was in his seat, not the lounge. This creates a spatial conflict: if Edgeworth was the only person in the lounge, he couldn't have seen Hicks who was elsewhere.

**Step 4: If none of the listed rules apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The contradiction is based on spatial presence. Testimony 3 asserts Edgeworth's continuous presence in the lounge. Evidence 9 establishes Hicks's presence elsewhere (his seat) at a specific time. These two statements are spatially incompatible; they cannot both be true simultaneously. The spatial reasoning involves understanding the location of individuals at different times and recognizing the conflict when those locations are mutually exclusive.

## OBJECTIVE:
**FIND THE CONTRADICTION:** Which evidence and testimony contradict each other?

## CONSTRAINT:
**You must only answer ONE PAIR.**

## ANALYSIS FRAMEWORK:
Please follow this format:

**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial reasoning rules** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable rules exist,** explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed rules apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

---

## CRITICAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT:
**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

Testimony 3 states: "The bloke wot owned the place was 'oldin' a gun, so 'e should 'ave just fired instead of yellin' at the girl!" This implies Windibank was holding the gun *before* the yelling and the gunshot. Testimony 2 states: "Just before the gunshot, we 'eard a voice yellin' out, 'Gimme that gun!'" This implies someone was demanding the gun *before* the gunshot. Evidence 10 (Crime Scene Photograph) and Evidence 9 (Autopsy Report) confirm the gunshot was to Windibank's back. This creates a spatial contradiction: if Windibank was holding the gun and yelling, he wouldn't have been shot in the back. The Skulkin brothers' testimony suggests Windibank was in a position to fire, but the autopsy report indicates he was shot from behind.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial reasoning rules apply**

The rule "If XXX is shot from YYY direction then the bullet comes from YYY direction" applies. In this case, XXX is Windibank, and YYY is "behind." The rule states the bullet *must* come from behind. Testimony 3 implies Windibank was holding the gun and therefore facing forward, which contradicts the bullet coming from behind.

**Step 3: If applicable rules exist, state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

The rule "If XXX is shot from YYY direction then the bullet comes from YYY direction" directly contradicts the narrative presented in Testimony 3. Testimony 3 suggests Windibank was holding the gun and potentially facing forward, implying he could have fired it. However, the autopsy report and crime scene photograph (Evidence 9 and 10) confirm he was shot from behind. This spatial contradiction reveals that Windibank could not have been holding the gun and facing forward at the time of the shooting, as the bullet came from behind.

**Step 4: If none of the listed rules apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

N/A - A relevant rule was found.

**Step 5:** {"evidence": 9, "testimony": 3}

  "testimony": 3

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):

**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

The contradiction lies between Testimony 2 ("While I was searching, I heard a scream coming from the next room over.") and Testimony 3 ("Although I was in the next room, I was unable to catch a glimpse of the Yatagarasu."). Shih-na claims to have been in the adjacent room while hearing a scream, yet simultaneously states she couldn't see the Yatagarasu. This implies a spatial inconsistency: if she was *in* the next room, she should have had a reasonable opportunity to see whoever was screaming or causing the commotion. The inability to see them despite being in the adjacent room suggests she wasn't actually in the next room.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial reasoning rules apply**

The rule "If XXX is in the middle of YYY then XXX is equidistant from all edges of YYY" doesn't apply here. The rule "If XXX touches YYY then YYY touches XXX" also doesn't apply. None of the other rules directly address the spatial relationship of being in an adjacent room and visibility.

**Step 3: N/A**

**Step 4: Spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The contradiction is identified through basic spatial reasoning and common sense. Being "in the next room over" implies proximity and a reasonable line of sight. The inability to see anything despite this proximity is inherently contradictory. It suggests Shih-na's location was not as close as she claims, or that something obstructed her view, which she hasn't mentioned.
